```markdown
Run a limited pilot with early users / one or more Arbitrum protocols, collect metrics, and publish a report.

Deliverables:

- Pilot metrics: number of policies sold, total premium volume, number of claims, loss ratio over the pilot period.
- Public post-mortem / report summarizing what worked, what didn’t, and next steps to scale (including audit plans).
```

### Are milestones clearly defined…? KPIs?  

你可以在一个文本框里这样总结：

```markdown
Yes.

Example KPIs per milestone:

- M1 (contracts + backend):
  - Contracts deployed on Arbitrum testnet and mainnet.
  - ≥ 95% unit test coverage for core contracts.
  - Backend reliably tracking 100% of PremiumPaid events.

- M2 (public alpha + dashboard):
  - Public dApp live on Arbitrum with ≥ 20 test users interacting.
  - Dashboard updating at least daily with policy counts and treasury data.

- M3 (pilot + report):
  - ≥ 100 policies sold during pilot.
  - Loss ratio tracked and published over a defined period.
  - At least 1 integration or co-marketing pilot with an Arbitrum protocol or community.
  - Public post-mortem report shared with the Arbitrum community.
```

---

### Estimated maximum time for completion  

```text
6 months
```

---

### How should the Arbitrum community measure the success of this grant?  

```markdown
Key metrics:

- Number of policies sold on Arbitrum during and after the pilot period.
- Total premium volume and number of valid claims paid.
- Loss ratio (payouts / premiums) over time.
- Number of Arbitrum protocols / communities integrating or promoting LiqPass.
- User retention and repeat purchases of cover.

Qualitative:
- Clarity and transparency of the dashboard and evidence model.
- Feedback from pilot partners about ease of integration and user experience.
```

---

### Economic plan after the grant period  

```markdown
Post-grant, LiqPass aims to be self-sustaining through:

- A risk load and protocol fee baked into premiums, funding ongoing development and operations.
- Revenue-sharing agreements with integrated Arbitrum protocols, communities, and aggregators.
- Additional integrations with perp, options, and structured-product protocols on Arbitrum to grow volume.
- Over time, potential participation in other Arbitrum ecosystem programs (e.g., audit subsidies, gas sponsorship) to improve safety and reduce friction.

The grant funds are used to reach a stable v1, after which LiqPass is designed to operate as a lean, fee-supported protocol with incremental upgrades.
```

## 四、Domain Specific Information（领域信息）

### Category  

在下拉里选类似：

```text
DeFi / New Protocol (Liquidation Insurance)
```

---

### Protocol Performance  

```markdown
On Arbitrum, LiqPass is pre-launch; performance metrics will be generated during this grant.

On another L2 (Base), a working MVP has been deployed and tested in a demo environment with:
- Checkout contract live and emitting premium payment events.
- Backend and verifier services successfully processing test claims.
- A front-end demo at https://wjz5788.com/ used for internal and early external feedback.

This grant will allow us to bring that functionality to Arbitrum, run pilots, and start publishing real usage metrics.
```

---

### Audit History & Security Vendors  

```markdown
No third-party audits yet.

Current status:
- Internal reviews and testing of core contracts.
- Event-driven accounting model designed to limit worst-case treasury exposure (per-day / per-user caps).

Plan:
- Apply to the Arbitrum Audit Program for a third-party audit once the Arbitrum contracts stabilize.
- Consider additional reviews / formal verification as usage grows.
- No bug bounty yet; we plan to introduce a small bug bounty after v1 is live and audited.
```

---

### Is your project composable with other projects on Arbitrum?  

```markdown
Yes.

LiqPass is designed as a composable primitive:
- Perp/derivatives protocols can integrate LiqPass at the UI level (offering optional cover at order placement) or at the smart contract level (e.g., bundling coverage with certain strategies).
- Aggregators and copy-trading platforms can route users into both positions and protection with a single flow.
- Wallets and dashboards can read LiqPass policy status onchain and surface it as part of a user’s portfolio view.

The protocol exposes clear onchain events (PremiumPaid, PolicyCreated, PayoutTriggered) and read methods so other Arbitrum projects can build on top of it.
```

---

### Is the proposal scope realistic and well-defined…?  

```markdown
Yes.

The scope is intentionally narrow: deliver a v1 of a liquidation insurance primitive on Arbitrum, with a public alpha and at least one pilot integration, within 6 months. The founder is already running an MVP on another L2 and has shipped all layers (contracts, backend, verifier, front-end), which reduces execution risk.

The milestones, budget, and timeline are aligned with a lean, solo-founder team and focus on shipping a minimal but usable product instead of an oversized roadmap.
```

---

### How did you find out about this program?  

```text
Arbitrum Twitter and Arbitrum DAO community announcements.
```

## Grant Information

### Title – Name of your Project
LiqPass DevKit – Verifiable Liquidation & Risk Tooling for Arbitrum & Stylus

---

### Project Details – Describe your project/idea, brief and concise

LiqPass DevKit is a **developer toolkit** for building **verifiable, event-driven risk modules** (liquidation insurance, parametric protection, “max loss” guards) on **Arbitrum One and Stylus**.

The toolkit provides:

- On-chain **Stylus/Solidity libraries** for verifying off-chain proofs (liquidation events, position PnL, risk triggers).  
- An **off-chain verifier service & SDK** (TypeScript/Rust) that ingests read-only data (exchange APIs / protocol subgraphs), normalizes it, builds **Merkle-rooted evidence**, and signs it.  
- **Frontend components** and example flows that make it easy for devs to plug a “verify & payout” flow into their dApp.

It’s currently live as an MVP on Base (demoing for high-leverage BTC traders); this grant is to **port and upgrade it as open-source dev tooling for Arbitrum & Stylus**, not just a single product.

---

### What innovation or value will your project bring to Arbitrum? What previously unaddressed problems is it solving? Is the project introducing genuinely new mechanisms?

**Problems today:**

- Many perps / derivatives / structured products lack **standard, verifiable tooling** for:
  - “Was this position liquidated?”  
  - “Did this risk trigger actually fire?”  
  - “Can we prove this to a third party (insurer, auditor, DAO)?”  
- Each team re-implements custom, fragile glue between **off-chain data** (CEX APIs, protocol APIs, subgraphs) and **on-chain logic** (payouts, risk modules).  
- There is no common, audited **evidence format + on-chain verification pattern** that other protocols can reuse.

**What LiqPass DevKit adds:**

- A **reusable evidence pipeline**: canonical JSON → Merkle tree → signed bundle → **Stylus / Solidity verifier** on Arbitrum.  
- **Parametric risk templates** (e.g. liquidation-triggered payout, drawdown-triggered payout) that any perps / structured-product protocol can fork instead of rebuilding from scratch.  
- A focus on **verifiability and auditability**: same inputs → same evidence root → reproducible decisions.

This is a **new mechanism** in the sense that it standardizes the path:

> off-chain trading data → deterministic, hash-committed proof → minimal on-chain check

and packages it as developer tooling for Arbitrum, instead of a closed insurance product.

---

### Do you have a target audience? If so, which one?

Primary target audience:

- **Developers** building on Arbitrum One and Stylus, especially:
  - **Perps / derivatives / options / structured products**
  - **Lending / leverage protocols** that want “max loss” or downside protection
  - **Risk managers / DAO treasuries** that need auditable triggers for payouts or circuit-breakers

Secondary:

- **Integrators & aggregators** (frontends, vault managers) who want to add “protected” strategies and need verifiable risk triggers.

In form options:  
- For protocols/ideas → **DeFi users, governance users, others (risk infra & dev tooling)**  
- For tools → **developers (TypeScript/Rust/Solidity/Stylus)**

---

### Team experience and completeness

We are currently a **lean, technical founder-driven team**, with some roles to be expanded as we grow usage on Arbitrum.

**Core roles**

- **Founder / Architect – zms**  
  - Designs the overall event-driven architecture: chain listener → verifier → evidence → payout.  
  - Experience shipping a working MVP on Base:  
    - CheckoutUSDC payment contract (mainnet)  
    - TypeScript/Express backend with SQLite, event-driven order reconciliation  
    - FastAPI verification service that talks to CEX APIs and emits Merkle-rooted evidence  
    - React front-end with wallet login & order/claim flows.

- **Smart Contract Engineer (to be hired/contracted with grant)**  
  - Responsibilities: Stylus modules, Solidity adapters, tests (fuzz/property), security-first design.  

- **Backend / DevTools Engineer (part-time now, expanded with grant)**  
  - Responsibilities: verifier service, SDKs (TS/Rust), logging & replayability, CI.

**Skillset**

- Event-driven architectures, verifiable logging, Merkle trees, deterministic JSON encoding.  
- Solidity smart contracts and EVM infra (deploy, verify, monitor).  
- TypeScript / Node.js backends, Python/FastAPI services.  
- Frontend UX for complex flows (insurance, claims, risk dashboards).

**References**

- Public codebase: https://github.com/wjz5788/LiqPass  
- Demo deployment on Base (MVP) with real contract and front-end flows.

We will **use grant funds to formalize** a smart-contract specialist and harden the toolkit for external users.

---

### Do you know about any comparable protocol, event, game, tool or project within the Arbitrum ecosystem?

There are **insurance / risk projects** and **oracle providers** on Arbitrum (e.g. coverage protocols, structured products, oracle services), but:

- They are typically **end-user products** or **oracles**, not a **general-purpose dev toolkit** for
  - building verifiable liquidation / risk triggers,
  - generating hash-committed evidence bundles,
  - and verifying them via Stylus / Solidity libraries.

LiqPass DevKit is complementary: it aims to be **the toolkit other teams use** to build their own protected products.

---

### What is the current stage of your project?

- **Overall LiqPass (on Base):** Working MVP –  
  - Smart contract deployed on Base mainnet  
  - Front-end demo live (wallet connect, buy policy, view orders)  
  - Back-end + verification services working in demo mode

- **Arbitrum & Stylus version (this grant):**  
  - **Architecture defined, prototype in progress**  
  - Need funding to:
    - Implement Stylus verifier contracts and Solidity adapters  
    - Ship open-source SDKs  
    - Integrate with at least one Arbitrum perps / DeFi protocol as a reference

---

### Have you received a grant from the DAO, Foundation, or any Arbitrum ecosystem related program or conducted any IRL like a hackathon or workshop?

No, we haven’t received any Arbitrum grants yet.  
We also haven’t run IRL Arbitrum-specific workshops yet; this grant will support our **first Arbitrum-focused tooling release and workshops**.

---

### Have you received a grant from any other entity in other blockchains that are not Arbitrum?

No.  
We are in early conversations with other ecosystems (e.g., Base grants), but **no grants have been awarded yet**. Development so far has been self-funded.

---

## Grant request details

### What is the idea/project for which you are applying for a grant? (Be detailed, including implementation plans)

We are applying to build **LiqPass DevKit for Arbitrum One & Stylus**:

A **developer-first toolkit** that makes it easy to:

1. **Ingest & normalize off-chain or protocol data**  
   - For perps / derivatives / lending protocols on Arbitrum or connected venues.  
   - Use a repeatable pipeline: fetch → normalize → canonical JSON → Merkle tree.

2. **Produce verifiable evidence bundles**  
   - Evidence includes: position id, symbol, leverage, timestamps, liquidation price, PnL, and any relevant risk metrics.  
   - The service computes a **Merkle root** and signs an “evidence header” with clear domain separation for Arbitrum.

3. **Verify proofs on-chain with Stylus / Solidity**  
   - Stylus module verifies:
     - Signature (attestor key / rotating keys)
     - Merkle inclusion proofs
     - Basic invariants (e.g. timestamp windows, replay protection).  
   - Solidity adapters expose simple interfaces (e.g. `verifyEvidenceRoot` and `settlePayout`).

4. **Provide ready-made parametric risk templates**  
   Examples:
   - “Liquidation protection”: if position was liquidated in coverage window → pay up to X% of loss.  
   - “Drawdown guard”: if PnL drawdown exceeds threshold within window → trigger compensation.  
   - “Max loss cap”: enforce hard bound on portfolio loss for a strategy vault.

5. **Deliver SDKs and sample integrations**  
   - TypeScript SDK to plug into existing backends.  
   - Rust helper library for Stylus integrations.  
   - Example integrations with at least one Arbitrum DeFi protocol (e.g. a perps / leverage / options protocol) and a demo app.

Implementation is staged into **three main phases** (see milestones below):  
1) Core verifier + Stylus contracts,  
2) SDKs + reference integration,  
3) Docs, examples, and workshops.

---

### Outline the major deliverables you will obtain with this grant (Be specific)

1. **Core Verifier & Stylus Contracts**
   - Stylus module implementing signature verification, Merkle proof checks, and replay protection.
   - Solidity wrapper contract exposing a clean interface for other protocols.
   - On-chain unit tests + fuzz/property tests.

2. **Verifier Service & SDKs**
   - Open-source verifier service (TypeScript or Rust backend) that:
     - Normalizes raw trading / protocol data.
     - Produces canonical JSON, Merkle trees, and signed evidence bundles.
   - TypeScript SDK for dApp / backend integration.
   - CLI tool for local testing (devs can run `liqpass verify <input.json>`).

3. **Reference Integration on Arbitrum One**
   - End-to-end demo integrating LiqPass DevKit with one Arbitrum protocol (e.g. perps / lending / structured product).  
   - Example flows: open position → simulate liquidation or drawdown → generate and verify evidence → trigger payout.

4. **Docs, Examples & Workshops**
   - Full documentation site:
     - architecture
     - evidence format
     - API+SDK docs
     - Stylus contract integration guide
   - Example repos:
     - “Minimal liquidation protection module”
     - “Orbit chain risk module template”
   - 1–2 online workshops / tutorials targeted at Arbitrum developers.

---

### Please explain how your idea/project aligns with the Arbitrum ecosystem goals and why it will serve as a growth force for Arbitrum.

**Alignment with Arbitrum goals:**

- **DeFi dominance**  
  - Safer, more transparent risk tooling → easier for users to trust complex products.  
  - Protocols can add “protected” versions of strategies, broadening their user base.  
  - Standardized evidence & payout flows reduce engineering cost and risk of new products.

- **Developer tools & Stylus adoption**  
  - Shows a **clear Stylus use-case**: compute-heavy verification of proofs and Merkle trees.  
  - Provides a **concrete, reusable codebase** devs can study and fork, instead of starting from a blank Stylus repo.

- **Ecosystem expansion & Orbit chains**  
  - Orbit chains can reuse the **same evidence format & verifier pattern**, making it easier to launch app-chains with strong risk tooling.  
  - The toolkit supports multi-chain evidence sources but anchors trust on Arbitrum, reinforcing Arbitrum’s role as risk settlement hub.

**Growth force:**

- Lowers the barrier for new **risk-aware protocols** to launch on Arbitrum.  
- Encourages existing protocols to **add new protected SKUs**, increasing TVL and volume.  
- Creates a **shared standard** and vocabulary for risk triggers, evidence, and payouts.

---

### What is your requested grant (USD)?

**Requested amount:** **25,000 USD**

This keeps the scope focused, with clear 3–4 month deliverables and measurable adoption targets.

---

### Detailed breakdown of the budget

Total: **25,000 USD**

- **Smart Contract & Stylus Engineering – 10,000 USD**  
  - Design & implementation of Stylus verifier module and Solidity adapters  
  - Unit tests, fuzz/property tests, basic security review

- **Backend & SDK Development – 7,000 USD**  
  - Verifier service (fetch/normalize/evidence)  
  - TypeScript SDK and CLI tooling  
  - Logging, replayability, basic monitoring

- **Frontend Examples & Documentation – 4,000 USD**  
  - Example dApp integration on Arbitrum testnet/mainnet  
  - Docs site, guides, code samples

- **Infra & DevOps – 2,000 USD**  
  - Test and staging infra (nodes, logging, monitoring)  
  - CI/CD, automated test pipelines

- **Community & Adoption – 2,000 USD**  
  - Time & resources for 1–2 online workshops / tutorials  
  - Example repos, demo content, developer support in early integrations

---

### Milestones, amounts, deliverables, and timelines

We plan to complete within **4–5 months**, under the 6-month requirement.

**Milestone 1 – Core Stylus Verifier & Solidity Adapter**  
- **Amount:** 8,000 USD  
- **Deliverables:**
  - Stylus module implementing:
    - signature verification with domain separation  
    - Merkle proof verification  
    - replay protection (nonces / windows)  
  - Solidity adapter contract with simple external interface (e.g. `verifyEvidenceRoot`)  
  - Unit tests + fuzz tests with example evidence bundles  
- **Estimated completion:** Month 1–2

**Milestone 2 – Verifier Service & SDKs**  
- **Amount:** 8,000 USD  
- **Deliverables:**
  - Open-source verifier service repository (TypeScript or Rust)  
  - Canonical JSON & evidence schema documented  
  - Merkle tree implementation with deterministic encoding  
  - TypeScript SDK + CLI for generating & verifying bundles locally  
- **Estimated completion:** Month 2–3

**Milestone 3 – Reference Integration on Arbitrum One**  
- **Amount:** 6,000 USD  
- **Deliverables:**
  - Integration with at least one Arbitrum DeFi protocol (testnet or mainnet; perps / leverage / structured)  
  - End-to-end demo: position open → liquidation/drawdown simulated → evidence generated → on-chain verification & payout call  
  - Metrics instrumentation (events/logs) for usage tracking  
- **Estimated completion:** Month 3–4

**Milestone 4 – Documentation, Examples & Workshops**  
- **Amount:** 3,000 USD  
- **Deliverables:**
  - Public docs site and example repos (minimal risk module, Orbit template)  
  - One or two online workshops / recorded tutorials for Arbitrum devs  
  - “Getting started” guides and troubleshooting playbook  
- **Estimated completion:** Month 4–5

---

### Are milestones clearly defined, time-bound, measurable? KPIs per milestone

Yes. Example KPIs:

- **Milestone 1**
  - Stylus & Solidity contracts deployed on Arbitrum testnet  
  - ≥ 90% line coverage in unit tests  
  - ≥ 20 property/fuzz tests for evidence verification

- **Milestone 2**
  - Verifier service and SDKs published as **open source**  
  - npm package published with ≥ 50 downloads in first month  
  - CLI able to deterministically produce identical evidence roots from the same inputs

- **Milestone 3**
  - At least **1 live reference integration** (testnet or mainnet)  
  - ≥ 100 successful verification calls in test/demo environment  
  - At least 1 protocol team confirms integration feasibility in a short case study

- **Milestone 4**
  - Docs site live, with ≥ 3 full integration guides  
  - ≥ 2 public examples (GitHub) using the toolkit  
  - Workshops with ≥ 20 unique developers attending / watching

---

### What is the estimated maximum time for the completion of the project?

**5 months**, comfortably under the 6-month cap.

---

### How should the Arbitrum community measure the success of this grant?

Key success metrics:

- **Developer adoption**
  - Number of projects integrating or piloting LiqPass DevKit  
  - GitHub stars/forks of the main repo  
  - NPM downloads of the SDK

- **On-chain usage**
  - Number of verification calls to the Stylus / Solidity verifier contracts  
  - Number of unique integrations (protocols using the toolkit in testnet/mainnet)

- **Ecosystem impact**
  - Number of new “protected” products or strategies launched on Arbitrum using the toolkit  
  - Feedback from integrator teams (dev surveys, short testimonials)

---

### What is the economic plan for maintaining operations or continuing the growth of your project after the grant period?

Post-grant, we plan to:

- Keep the **core toolkit fully open source** to maximize adoption.  
- Monetize via:
  - **Integration & support agreements** with protocols that want help customizing risk modules / evidence flows.  
  - Optional **hosted verifier / monitoring service** for teams that don’t want to run infra themselves (SaaS-like).  
  - Potential **rev-share** on specific protected SKUs co-launched with partner protocols.

We will also:

- Apply for / collaborate with **specific protocols, DAOs, and other ecosystems** for co-funded integrations.  
- Continue building the codebase as a core piece of our long-term product roadmap (LiqPass as both toolkit + end-user products where appropriate).

---

## Milestones and Timelines (form section)

## Domain Specific Information

**Category**  
Developer Tooling – Risk & Verification Infra for DeFi on Arbitrum One & Stylus

---

**Market Needs: For developers building apps and interfaces, what specific challenges or opportunities do they face in Arbitrum?**

Challenges:

- Hard to **prove** external events (liquidations, PnL thresholds, risk triggers) in a standardized, auditable way.  
- Each team either trusts its own backend or builds ad-hoc verification logic, which is fragile and non-portable.  
- There is a lack of **ready-made, audited patterns** for bridging off-chain data and on-chain payouts.

Opportunities:

- Arbitrum’s DeFi ecosystem is rich with perps, options, structured products, and yield strategies. They all benefit from:
  - standardized risk tooling,  
  - better UX around “protected” trades,  
  - shared libraries instead of bespoke glue code.  

LiqPass DevKit directly targets these needs by giving developers a **drop-in toolkit** for verifiable, event-driven risk logic.

---

**Composability: How can your proposed developer tooling facilitate seamless interaction between different protocols and tools built on Arbitrum, the Nova ecosystem and other Orbit chains?**

- The **evidence format and verifier pattern are protocol-agnostic**:
  - Any protocol that can expose its state (subgraph, API, indexer) can plug into the verifier service.  
- The **on-chain verifier** is a small, composable module:
  - Other contracts can call it as a library or via a simple “verify & consume” interface.  
- For **Nova and Orbit chains**, we can:
  - Share the **same evidence schema** and **Merkle proof format**,  
  - Deploy verifier modules on each chain,  
  - Or settle risk on Arbitrum while applications run on Orbit chains.

This enables a network of protocols, vaults, and frontends all speaking the **same “evidence language”**, making composability natural.

---

**Adoption: If your grant focuses on a Growth or Orbit Chain developer tool, how would you ensure its widespread adoption within the developer community?**

Our adoption strategy:

- **Open-source everything** (permissive license) and keep the repo clean, documented, and easy to fork.  
- Provide **high-quality examples**:
  - minimal reference integration  
  - Orbit chain template  
  - copy-paste snippets for common patterns.  
- Run **workshops & office hours** with Arbitrum dev communities (Discord, forums, hackathons).  
- Collaborate with **at least one anchor protocol** as an early design partner to validate and showcase the toolkit.  
- Maintain a **simple, stable API and schema**, with versioning, so integrators feel safe building on top.

---

**If working on Orbit Chain Onboarding and Tooling: How would you adapt your existing developer tooling (or propose a new tool) to simplify building applications on custom Arbitrum Layer 3 chains (Orbit Chains)?**

For Orbit chains:

- Keep the **evidence and proof logic chain-agnostic**, and let Orbit apps:
  - either verify proofs locally on their Orbit chain,  
  - or rely on Arbitrum as a **risk settlement hub** that verifies and records outcomes.  
- Provide:
  - an **Orbit chain boilerplate** (contracts + config) to consume LiqPass DevKit proofs,  
  - deployment scripts to spin up a verifier module alongside an Orbit app.  
- Document best practices for:
  - how Orbit apps should expose their state to the verifier service,  
  - how to use Orbit for app-specific logic while anchoring key risk decisions on Arbitrum.

---

**If applying for a growth oriented grant: Please provide success metrics for the grant with milestone-oriented disbursements**

(Not strictly growth-only, but growth metrics we’ll track:)

- Number of **integrating protocols** (testnet + mainnet) using the toolkit.  
- Number of **verification calls** on Arbitrum contracts.  
- GitHub repo metrics: stars, forks, PRs from external teams.  
- Workshop engagement: attendees, number of teams that start pilots afterwards.

---

## Other information

**How did you find out about this program?**  
Arbitrum Twitter and Questbook grants portal.

**Stay Updated – Get the latest updates about grants**  
Yes – happy to receive updates.

---

这版已经尽量把 LiqPass 包装成一个「给 Arbitrum + Stylus 用的开发工具」，而不是只是一款 C 端保险产品。你只需要把带 `[]` 的地方改成你自己的真实信息，然后按需要删减一些细节就可以直接提交。

### AI
<!-- mid: 85be48bb-21b7-4458-a57d-ccc053959b43 -->
行，我懂你意思：  
现在这个更像「月球小夜灯 + 木头摆件」，科技感不够、逻辑也说不通。

我分两块：**1）长什么样更「科」一点，2）它到底怎么漂浮而又不影响 Wi-Fi。**

---

## 1. 先把造型救一下：从“月亮灯”变成“信号行星”

### 月球本体（别再是土土的球灯）

- 不要写实坑坑洼洼那种“淘宝爆款月球灯”，  
  改成 **半磨砂、淡银灰的玻璃/亚克力球体**，表面只有非常微弱的“月海纹理 + 线路感刻纹”，远看是月亮，近看是主板走线。
- 光：**冷白 + 轻微蓝色边缘光**，亮度低，只做氛围，不抢 Wi-Fi 指示灯的戏。
- 球体里可以有一个隐约可见的「核心」：像一颗悬浮的小白点/光芯，让人感觉里面有计算单元。

### 金属环（从戒指变成轨道）

- 做成 **更细的深色钛金属环**，表面是极小的点状灯珠，像卫星轨道灯带。
- 灯效不要“闪烁”，改成 **缓慢流动的「信号脉冲」**：  
  冷白为主，偶尔一小段金色光点沿轨道滑过，像数据包在跑。

### 路由器本体（底座）

- 底座改成 **扁平的六边形 / 八边形碟形路由器**：
  - 上表面是深色拉丝金属或陶瓷涂层。
  - 侧边留一圈 **细细的状态灯条**（Wi-Fi、WAN、系统…），用极简图标表示。
  - 进风/出风孔做在底部 + 背部，不破坏整体极简感。
- 颜色：深钛灰 / 石墨黑，比胡桃木更“设备”，木头可以只做下面那块石材桌、或者底部点缀。

一句话：上面是**“信号行星”**，下面是**“指挥舰桥”**，视觉上先把它从「摆件」变成「核心设备」。

---

## 2. 它到底怎么漂浮，又不影响信号？

### 方案 A：真实磁悬浮 + 纯装饰球（推荐）

逻辑这样拆：

1. **路由器主板 + Wi-Fi 天线**  
   - 全部放在底座里，上半部分靠近侧边一圈，做成 **环形 PCB + 内置天线**。  
   - 天线朝水平方向和略微上方发射信号，不指着正上方中间那一点。

2. **悬浮系统**  
   - 在底座的几何中心，有一套 **电磁悬浮线圈 + 位置传感器**，只负责托着上面的“月球壳”。
   - 月球内部是一块小磁铁 + 轻量框架，外壳塑料/玻璃，不用金属罩，这样对 2.4/5 GHz Wi-Fi 几乎没有遮挡。
   - 电源用 **感应供电线圈**，从底座把功率射给球体里的接收线圈，给球体里的 LED 供电 → 不用线缆。

3. **对 Wi-Fi 的影响怎么控制？**

   - **静态磁场不挡 Wi-Fi**：Wi-Fi 是 GHz 级电磁波，磁悬浮的永磁体基本不会“挡信号”。  
   - 真正可能有干扰的是悬浮控制电路的开关噪声，这个可以：
     - 用金属屏蔽罩把悬浮驱动电路包起来；
     - 悬浮线圈和 Wi-Fi 天线**水平错层**布置，中间再加一圈接地铜环，做 EMI 隔离。
   - 月球壳体用 **塑料/玻璃 + 点状金属镀层（非完整金属壳）**，既有质感，又不形成完整的“法拉第笼”。

总结：  
**球是纯灯，底座才是路由器。磁悬浮只动球，不碰射频。**

---

### 方案 B：视觉悬浮（更便宜，更稳）

如果量产成本、可靠性更重要，可以考虑“视觉上看是悬浮的”，实际上有支点：

- 用一根 **非常细的深色金属支架** 从后面托起球体（和窗外夜景融到一起就几乎看不见）。
- 或者用一块 **透明亚克力楔形支柱**，正面看像漂浮，侧面才看得到支撑。
- 路由器主板和天线布局跟上面方案一样，支撑件尽量避免包住天线区域，不用大面积金属。

这套对 Wi-Fi 几乎没有任何技术风险，就是没有真·悬浮那么酷，但胜在好做、好卖、也不怕球掉下来砸人。

---

## 3. 给你一个新图的英文 Prompt（科技感版）

你可以直接拿去让模型再画一版：

> A hyper-minimal, futuristic Wi-Fi router that looks like a levitating "signal moon".  
> A semi-translucent, matte glass sphere with faint lunar textures and subtle circuit-like engravings hovers a few centimeters above an ultra-thin hexagonal router base.  
> The sphere emits a very soft cool white glow, with a razor-thin dark titanium orbital ring around it, where tiny LEDs create slow-moving data-pulse animations.  
> The router base is a low, graphite-black polygon with a clean light strip status bar along the front edge and hidden ventilation.  
> No visible antennas or wires.  
> The whole piece sits on a stone side table in a high-tech penthouse living room at night, with a blurred city skyline outside the panoramic windows.  
> Strong sci-fi industrial design, museum-grade product photo, 8K, ultra realistic, global illumination.

你后面要做「古典美学版」的时候，可以在这个结构上，把材质换成木、铜、纸灯、纹样之类，但核心逻辑不变：**路由器在底座，漂浮的是“信号图腾”，磁悬浮/视觉悬浮都只碰灯，不碰射频**。

行，我们先别心软，直接说这张图哪儿丑，然后给你一版**更科技、更高级的新 Prompt**。  

### 1. 这张图丑在哪儿

- **月亮太“淘宝夜灯”**：纹理太写实、太均匀，一眼就是便宜月球灯，不像高端装置。
- **颜色太黄、太暖**：暖白偏黄 + 大面积发光 → 视觉上“土”、“廉价”，缺少科技感。
- **光环太粗、太规整**：像项圈，不像雕塑；缺少那种“精密仪器”的锋利感。
- **底座又矮又钝**：没有“设备感”，更像一个纸镇，撑不起“智能家居中心”的叙事。

简单说：它更像**治愈系小夜灯**，不是**科技艺术装置**。

---

### 2. 方向调整：冷感 + 工业感 + 极简

我们改三件事：

1. **月球→抽象月球芯**  
   - 半磨砂、偏银灰的玻璃球，细腻纹理，但不那么写实。  
   - 只保留一点点月海暗影，远看是月亮，近看是电路+云雾。

2. **光从“面光”→“边缘光 / 核心光”**  
   - 球整体不强光，只在边缘和内部核心有很淡的冷白/微蓝光。  
   - 这样既高级，又不会显得像台灯。

3. **底座从“木疙瘩”→“科技器件”**  
   - 深色胡桃木可以保留，但做**超薄多边形盘+黑色金属腰线**。  
   - 在正面藏一条非常细的状态灯条，暗示“智能设备”。

### 3. 改过的英文 Prompt（偏科技、冷感、高级）

你可以直接用下面这个替换原来的 Prompt 再生一版：

> A hyper-realistic, ultra-minimal smart home hub that looks like an abstract “moon core”.  
> A semi-translucent, matte glass sphere with very subtle, stylized lunar textures and faint circuit-like engravings, softly levitating about one inch above a thin, dark walnut wood base with a sharp geometric cut design and a slim black metal accent band.  
> The sphere does **not** glow brightly; instead, it has a very faint cool white edge glow with a hint of blue, and a tiny inner core light, creating a calm, high-tech aura.  
> An extremely thin, dark titanium ring passes through the sphere, like an orbital path, with tiny LEDs forming slow, irregular golden data pulses.  
> No visible wires or antennas.  
> The object sits on a stone side table in an ultra-modern luxury penthouse living room with floor-to-ceiling panoramic windows and a softly blurred city skyline at night.  
> Industrial sci-fi product design, museum-grade art piece, photographed like a high-end consumer device, 8K, ultra realistic, global illumination, soft cinematic lighting.

---

如果这一版你仍觉得不够“科技狠活”，下一步我们可以直接把“月亮”再抽象一级：做成**空心圆环 + 内部悬浮小光核**，完全脱离“淘宝月球灯”的审美。

### 我
<!-- mid: 1c06f428-aa98-433d-a925-747871d431c1 -->
A hyper-realistic, ultra-minimal smart home hub that looks like an abstract “moon core”.
A semi-translucent, matte glass sphere with very subtle, stylized lunar textures and faint circuit-like engravings, softly levitating about one inch above a thin, dark walnut wood base with a sharp geometric cut design and a slim black metal accent band.
The sphere does not glow brightly; instead, it has a very faint cool white edge glow with a hint of blue, and a tiny inner core light, creating a calm, high-tech aura.
An extremely thin, dark titanium ring passes through the sphere, like an orbital path, with tiny LEDs forming slow, irregular golden data pulses.
No visible wires or antennas.
The object sits on a stone side table in an ultra-modern luxury penthouse living room with floor-to-ceiling panoramic windows and a softly blurred city skyline at night.
Industrial sci-fi product design, museum-grade art piece, photographed like a high-end consumer device, 8K, ultra realistic, global illumination, soft cinematic lighting.

<!-- mid: 026dbae4-6628-48ef-b0f5-188ec964062e -->
好，这个方向很爽：  
**“上古神话皮肤 + 现代路由器内核”**，做成一整套系列。

我给你设计一组世界观 + 4 款外壳，每款附中英文描述和可直接用的生图 Prompt。

---

## 系列世界观：  
**《山海光路 · Ancient Myths Router Series》**

- 核心概念：  
  - 上古神话里追日、驭水、渡河、观星的角色，其实都是“带宽拟人化”  
  - 他们追的不是太阳，是**光速**；扛的不是石头，是**数据包**  
- 形式：  
  - 内部是同一颗高性能路由器主板  
  - 外壳是不同神话主题的“雕塑外骨骼”  
  - 放家里既是艺术手办，又是核心网络设备  

---

## 1. 夸父逐日 · 「逐光路由器」

**设计概念（中文）**

- 造型：  
  - 整体是一个略前倾的夸父剪影浮雕，融进路由器外壳的上半部分  
  - 人物不是完整写实，而是**几何切面 + 青铜纹样**的混合  
- 动势：  
  - 外壳前方向上延伸一条淡金色“光轨”，像太阳轨迹，又像数据流动线路  
- 路由器本体：  
  - 下半部分是扁平圆角长方体，前方有一条极细状态灯线  
  - 顶部微微向前倾，像夸父在迎着风跑  

**英文生图 Prompt**

> A high-end Wi-Fi router whose outer shell is inspired by the ancient Chinese myth of Kuafu chasing the sun.  
> The device is a low, sleek rectangular base with hidden antennas and a thin front status light bar.  
> Rising from the top is a semi-abstract, faceted bronze-like relief of a giant striding forward, his body formed by geometric planes and engraved cloud patterns, leaning slightly as if running.  
> From his outstretched arm, a thin golden light trail curves over the router surface like both a sun path and a data stream.  
> Materials: matte dark metal, aged bronze accents, subtle warm gold glow lines.  
> No visible cables. Placed on a stone tabletop in a modern living room with soft, indirect lighting.  
> Ultra-detailed product photo, 8K, cinematic, museum-grade industrial design.

---

## 2. 长河奔域 · 「奔流路由器」

**设计概念（中文）**

- 关键词：**长河、奔涌、带宽洪流、分叉支流 = 多频多路**  
- 造型：  
  - 外壳顶部整体是一个**下切的河谷形轮廓**，像是水流刻出的路径  
  - 侧面有连续起伏的波纹浮雕，内部嵌入极细的蓝白光纤灯带  
- 细节：  
  - 前侧有一条“主干河道”灯带，分叉成几条极细支流，象征多路数据通道  

**英文生图 Prompt**

> A futuristic Wi-Fi router with an outer shell themed around a “surging long river”.  
> The base is a low, wide polygon with smoothed edges in matte dark stone-like material.  
> The top surface is carved into a flowing river valley shape, with layered wave-like ridges.  
> Along these ridges, ultra-thin embedded light strips in cool white and deep blue create the impression of water and high-speed data streams branching into multiple channels.  
> No visible antennas; all radio hardware is hidden inside.  
> The overall form should look like a miniature landscape sculpture and a high-end network device at the same time.  
> Placed on a walnut console in a dim penthouse interior with a night cityscape outside.  
> Hyper-realistic product photography, global illumination, subtle reflections.

---

## 3. 「跨海星桥」 · 远古渡海 / 光纤跨洋

**设计概念（中文）**

- 灵感：上古神话中的“渡海”“踏浪” + 现代跨洋光缆  
- 造型：  
  - 下层是深色海面 —— 光滑曲面，上有微微起伏  
  - 上层是一座极简“弧形星桥”，从一侧岸岩连接到另一侧  
  - 桥底和桥面边缘跑着极细的光点，像光纤和星尘  

**英文生图 Prompt**

> A high-end Wi-Fi router whose outer casing is designed as an “ancient cosmic sea bridge”.  
> The device base is a smooth, dark glossy surface shaped like a calm ocean with slight waves.  
> Emerging diagonally across the router is a minimal, arc-shaped bridge structure in brushed metal, connecting two small rocky islets integrated into the shell.  
> Along the underside and edges of the bridge, ultra-small LEDs form a slow-moving band of light, evoking submarine fiber cables carrying data across oceans.  
> No external antennas.  
> The overall silhouette should feel like an ancient mythic crossing and a futuristic network backbone at once.  
> Displayed on a stone table in a softly lit, modern study, with hints of starry sky reflected in nearby glass.  
> Ultra-realistic, museum-quality product shot.

---

## 4. 「山海界域」 · 边界、信号覆盖范围

**设计概念（中文）**

- 灵感：**《山海经》+ 网络覆盖“域”**  
- 造型：  
  - 外壳做成一个低矮的“山海地形模型”，四角微翘，中间是高地  
  - 山体用抽象几何块表示，边缘刻有细小符号（像古文又像电路标记）  
  - 山顶有一小块平面，光线从上向下扩散，代表信号覆盖全域  

**英文生图 Prompt**

> A Wi-Fi router designed as an abstract “Shan-Hai boundary map”.  
> The casing is a low, square platform whose top surface forms a miniature, stylized landscape: angular mountains, valleys, and coastlines in layered matte stone and dark metal.  
> Tiny engraved symbols along the ridges look halfway between ancient script and PCB markings.  
> At the central peak, a small flat facet emits a soft, radial glow that subtly washes over the surrounding terrain, symbolizing wireless coverage over different domains.  
> No visible antennas or cables.  
> Shot as a high-end art object on a neutral plinth, with controlled studio lighting and a dark gradient background, 8K, hyper-detailed.

---

## 怎么用这组设定

- 视觉统一：  
  - 同一系列：尺寸相近、底部接口区统一（背面 I/O 区），只是“上半身皮肤”完全不同。  
- 文案话术：  
  - 「上古神话里的神不是神，是你的带宽守护者。」  
  - 「逐日是追光速，治水是治丢包。」  

你后面要做**红楼梦古典审美版**，也可以沿用同一逻辑：  
每一款外壳是一段意象（大观园、潇湘馆、太虚幻境），内核都是同一颗极现代的“网关大脑”。

---

我帮你细化一下改版方向，让后面生成更接近你脑子里的画面：

### 1. 地台 / 脚下造型怎么改

现在：  
脚下更像一个普通的光滑底座，只是略有起伏。

你想要的：  
**夸父是从“山海”里跨出来的**，地台本身就应该是一块小型地形。

下次生图 Prompt 可以强调：

- “The base is sculpted as rugged mountains and a winding river, like a miniature landscape from ancient Chinese mythology, not a flat plinth.”
- “Kuafu’s feet are firmly stepping on jagged peaks and river valleys, as if striding across great mountains and rivers.”

中文版给自己记一下：

> 底座不是平板，而是一整块“小型山海地形”，有 **明显起伏的山脉轮廓 + 一两条弯曲的河道**，夸父脚下是山峰而不是光滑平面。

### 2. 角色颜色 / 材质怎么调

现在：  
整体偏暖金+咖啡色，感觉有点像现代雕塑馆，而不是上古青铜器那种味道。

你想要的更接近：

- **深色、压一点的“青铜 + 锈迹 + 暗金边”**
- 表面有冷绿、黯青的氧化感，只在边缘和光轨位置有暖金高光

Prompt 里可以改成：

- “Material: aged dark bronze with subtle turquoise patina, only edges catching a faint warm gold highlight.”
- “Less saturated orange, more like ancient ritual bronze with weathered, mythic feel.”

中文记要：

> 整体是 **深青铜色 + 轻微绿锈质感**，不是黄灿灿的金色；只有光轨和轮廓边缘有一点暖金高光，显得更古、更多“祭器”感。

好，那直接上「夸父 · 逐光路由器」Prompt v2，专门解决你说的**脚下要山河**、**颜色更古**这两个点。

---

### Kuafu “Light-Chaser Router” · Prompt v2 (English)

A hyper-realistic product shot of a high-end Wi-Fi router whose outer shell is inspired by the ancient Chinese myth of Kuafu chasing the sun.  
The device has a low, sleek rectangular router base with rounded edges, hidden antennas, a single thin front status light bar, and all I/O ports on the back, almost invisible from the camera angle.

The entire top of the base is sculpted as a miniature mythic landscape, not a flat plinth: rugged angular mountains, sharp ridges, and a winding river valley carved into the surface, like great mountains and rivers from an ancient legend.  
Kuafu’s running feet step firmly on these jagged peaks and valleys, as if he is striding across mountains and rivers at high speed.

Rising from this landscape is a semi-abstract statue of Kuafu: a giant hero in mid-stride, leaning forward as he runs.  
His body is formed by faceted geometric planes and engraved swirling cloud patterns that resemble ancient Chinese ornament.  
The overall material is **aged dark bronze** with a subtle turquoise patina, like an old ritual bronze; only the edges and high points catch a faint warm gold highlight.  
No bright orange metal, the palette is deep, muted, and ancient.

From Kuafu’s outstretched arm, a thin golden energy ribbon arcs across the air and skims over the mountain base, like both the path of the sun and a high-speed data stream.  
Fine glowing lines subtly trace along the river bed and some ridge lines, suggesting data flowing through the terrain.

No visible cables or antennas.  
The router sculpture sits on a stone or dark wood tabletop in a modern living room, softly lit with warm directional light, shallow depth of field, ultra-detailed 8K cinematic, museum-grade industrial design photography.

---

你后面可以拿这个 v2 当“母 Prompt”，再往下做几版变体，比如：

- 把 “mountains and a winding river valley” 换成 “towering cliffs and a crashing waterfall” → 逐日 + 落差感更强  
- 或者加一句 “more dramatic mountains, even more exaggerated vertical height” → 把脚下山拉高一截，人物更像从山海里蹬出来

这样整套「山海光路」系列就能统一在“神踏山河 + 现代路由器内核”这条视觉主轴上。

<!-- mid: 1203bb49-a4d7-4dec-8d50-670f99845fe1 -->
懂了，这才对味。  
逻辑就是：**这是个上古神话雕塑，恰好里面塞了一块路由器板子**，而不是“路由器上面插了个雕塑”。

给你一段「全系列通用限制尾巴」，以后你每个英文 Prompt 结尾都可以直接粘上，强行把塑料壳和灯赶出去：

---

**Series Constraint Suffix (English, copy-paste)**

This object should not look like a commercial plastic router shell.  
The entire outer form is a single integrated art sculpture, as if priced like a collectible art piece.  
No visible plastic, no glossy consumer electronics shell, no exposed antennas, no logo, and no separate LED light bar on the front.  
All electronic components, ports, and indicators are completely hidden inside the base, invisible from the outside.  
The material language is stone, metal, wood, or aged bronze, with a consistent artistic finish from top to bottom.

---

中文给你做个“心里锚”：

- 外面只允许出现：**石头 / 金属 / 木头 / 青铜** 这些“艺术品材料”  
- **看不见任何接口、灯、塑料、天线、logo**  
- 默认假设：电路板是从底部塞进去的，跟雕塑融为一体

后面你做「长河奔域」「跨海星桥」「山海界域」，都可以在各自的场景描述后面加这段尾巴，就能锁住“纯艺术外壳 + 隐藏路由器内核”的统一气质。
